# Firmware symbol map (E-M10 Mark I, body fw 1.4)

Master index of **named / confirmed** runtime symbols recovered by static RE of the decoded firmware
container (`OLY_E_113_1400_0000_0000.BIN`). One place to look up "what lives at address X" and to see
coverage. Per-subsystem detail lives in the linked docs; this file is the cross-reference.

- **Runtime base = `0x7d991020`.** Block-0 code/data resolve at `fileoff + 0x7d991020` (NOT the
  container's stated load `0x40300000`; xrefs only resolve against `0x7d991020`). See
  `docs/firmware/memory-map.md`, `docs/firmware/flash-storage.md`.
- Architecture: Panasonic MN10300 / AM33. Immediates render **decimal** in our objdump build.
- Regenerate the full address→doc index with `python3 -I tools/symbol_index.py` (or
  `--once 0x7de57532` to locate one address). This curated table is the important subset.

## Memory / layout anchors
| address | symbol | role | source |
|---|---|---|---|
| `0x7d991020` | `RUNTIME_BASE` | block-0 exec/reset base; reset entry | `memory-map.md`, `flash-storage.md` |
| `0x40300000` | `BLK0_LOAD` | container packaging load addr of block 0 (not exec base) | `container-format.md`, `blocks.md` |
| `0x41500000` | `BLK1_LOAD` | container load addr of block 1 | `blocks.md` |
| `0x42d00000` | `BLK2_LOAD` | container load addr of block 2 | `blocks.md` |
| `0x4035fb66` | `TASK_NAME_TBL` | id→name table, 145 records, 26-byte stride | `rtos-tasks.md` |
| `0x7d9a9384` | `TASK_CREATE_TBL` | task-creation descriptors, 134 records, 28-byte stride (`+0` id, `+12` fn, `+16` prio, `+20` stack) | `rtos-tasks.md` |
| `0x6000xxxx` | `FIRMUP_SCRATCH` | firmup RAM scratch (`0x60001130`, `0x6044cbc4`+) | `flash-storage.md`, `camera-fw-update.md` |
| `0xa0000000`–`0xa0fc7860` | `GLOBAL_DATA` | main SDRAM `.data`/`.bss` + global pointer cells (2447 distinct refs in block 0, dense at `0xa0a3`–`0xa0a9`); cached DRAM data segment | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0xa0a5c970` | `descriptor_gate_cell` | global pointer cell read by the ring-feature descriptor gate (`flags_offset 560`, `flag_mask 128`) | `community-dbfw-tool.md` |

## Firmware-update / flash path
| address | symbol | role | source |
|---|---|---|---|
| `0x7de57e24` | `firmup_validate` | top of pre-flash image validator (fail-closed) | `camera-fw-update.md` |
| `0x7da8883e` | `region_table_parse` | walks ≤32-record region table; size-sum check (err `0x3321`) | `camera-fw-update.md` |
| `0x7da88820` | `rgn_hdr_get+8` | region-header accessor: `u32 @+8` (data ptr/fieldA) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7da88826` | `rgn_hdr_get+12` | region-header accessor: `u32 @+12` (payload length) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7da88838` | `rgn_hdr_get+22` | region-header accessor: `u8 @+22` (type selector) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7de58985` | `firmup_check_id` | image-id gate: id byte `== 0x45 'E'` (via `0x7da8880b`) | `camera-fw-update.md` |
| `0x7de573d1` | `usr_checksum` | header↔tail 20-byte duplicate-array check (err `0x3202`/`0x3204`); NOT a payload hash | `camera-fw-update.md`, `patching-pipeline.md` |
| `0x7de58970` | `TYPE_FIRMUP_WRITE_OK` | validation-passed → begin write | `camera-fw-update.md` |
| `0x7de57dd1` | `SUCCESS_REBOOT` | post-flash success/reboot | `camera-fw-update.md` |
| `0x7d9cc860` | `firmup_apply_dispatch` | 9-slot component-descriptor apply loop | `flash-storage.md` |
| `0x7d9ca000`–`0x7d9cd900` | `fmem_phys_leaf` | physical NAND read/erase/program (BML/STL) | `flash-storage.md` |
| `0x7d9cdaaa` | `flash_err_handler` | common write/erase error bail | `flash-storage.md` |
| `0x7e21d6dc` | `startFirmUp` | USB-link firmware-update entry | `usb-ptp.md`, `camera-fw-update.md` |

## Hottest helpers (by block-0 call count) — global registry / accessors
Ranked by direct `call` xrefs; these dominate the call graph, so naming them makes most disassembly
readable.
| address | xrefs | symbol | role | source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `0x7e207498` | 12294 | `obj_get_f104` | field getter: `return *(a0+104)` (hottest single call target — `+104` is a very hot object field) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e7074a1` | 60 | `wifi_http_send_response` | **Wi-Fi CGI HTTP response emitter** — sends the HTTP response header then (optionally) the body over the camera's web-server socket. Self-named by its two error strings `"[WIFI_HTTP_UTL] wifi_release_utl_http_send_head ERROR ret="` (`0x7e77f210`) and `"...send_body ERROR ret="` (`0x7e77f24b`). `wifi_http_send_response(a0=conn ctx, d0=u16 head/status code, a1=body ptr, (32,sp)=body len)`: (1) `send_head 0x7e70752b(d0, flag=1)` → if `-1` log head-error via `0x7d9999f9` and return `-1`; (2) if body len≠0 **and** body ptr≠0, `send_body 0x7e70755d(ctx, len, body)` → if `-1` log body-error; return status. Module `0x7e70` = the **camera's Wi-Fi HTTP/CGI server** (rodata: `[WIFI_HTTP_UTL]`, `[WIFI_REMOCON_CGI]`, `REMO_CGI`, `<?xml version="1.0"?>`, `<response>`, `<affocus>`/`<afframesize>`, `OLYMPUSAP`, `PRM_WIFI_CHANNEL`, `passphrase`/`wep key`, `wlan_event_receive link up/down`) — the server side of the [`wifi-cgi.md`]/[`wifi-api.md`] endpoints. All **60 callers in-module `0x7e70`** — every CGI handler emits its reply through this. Callees: `send_head 0x7e70752b`, `send_body 0x7e70755d`, chunk path `0x7e70756f`, socket send `0x7d996fc6`, error-log `0x7d9999f9`. **Dead end:** exact encoding of the u16 head code (HTTP status vs internal content-type enum) not pinned | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e7c910f` | 60 | `camprop_desc_lookup` | **Camera-property descriptor lookup by property-id** — shared registry behind both the USB-PTP engine (module `0x7e7c` hosts `PC_PTP`/`PTPASYNC` tasks) and the **Wi-Fi CGI `get_camprop`/`set_camprop`** handlers (dominant caller module `0x7e71` = Wi-Fi remote-control: strings `DREQUEST.DPS`/`DDISCVRY.DPS`, `[WIFI_REMOCON_THROUGH]`, `<propname>`/`<value>`/`<enum>`, `ext.af.top_x`/`width`/`color`). `camprop_desc_lookup(d0=prop_id) → desc* or 0`: `id==0` → 0; registry-root/init guard `*(0x6046372c)` null → 0; **`id < 1024`** → direct path `0x7e7c913c`; **`id ≥ 1024`** → banded search over 24-byte band descriptors (table `0x6046371C`, ≤3–4 bands keyed by first-u32 upper bound) → per-band locked matcher **`0x7e7c9192`**. The matcher buckets the key by stride `*(desc)` (`divu`), bounds-checks band count `*(desc+8)`, indexes the sub-entry array `*(desc+20)` (`+d2*4`), all under **E_* semaphore `*(u16)0x7d9b0396`** (`OlySemaphoreWait 0x7d9c1606`). Band-descriptor layout: `+0 stride/divisor, +8 count, +12 idx, +16 ?, +20 array_ptr`. Globals: **`0x6046372c` = registry root/ready flag**, band tables at **`0x6046371C`** (<1024) / **`0x6046377C`** (≥1024). 60 callers: 41×`0x7e71` (Wi-Fi CGI camprop), 19×`0x7e7c` (PTP engine). Cross-links [`wifi-cgi.md`] get/set_camprop and the PTP property surface. **Dead end:** no name strings on the tables (binary descriptors) — exact prop-id→name map and the `<1024` vs `≥1024` split meaning not statically pinned | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d99263d` | 42 | `memcpy_aligned` | **`memcpy_aligned(d0=byte count, a0=dst, a1=src)` — alignment-optimized block copy that picks the widest aligned transfer unit.** Tests bit0 (`btst 1`) of dst, src and count: if any is odd → byte loop (`movbu`, `inc`, `count` iterations). Else tests bit1 (`btst 2`): if any misaligned at 2 → halfword loop (`movhu`, `+2`, `count>>1` iterations). Else → word loop (`mov (a1),d1` / `inc4`, `count>>2` iterations). Each loop uses the MN10300 hw-loop (`setlb`/`lcs`). Returns `retf [d2,a2]`. A libc-style `memcpy` **distinct from the OS-ROM `0x47001000` memcpy** — this is the in-firmware runtime copy. **Sibling at `0x7d992699` = matching `memset`** (fills `d0` byte across `d1`/`a0`, same byte/half/word alignment split). Bank `0x7d99` = core OS/runtime (just above base `0x7d991020`). **42 callers, kernel-region: 32×`0x7d99`, 6×`0x7d9c` (OlyQe), 4×`0x7d9d`.** | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e0547df` | 42 | `obj_scoped_call` | **`obj_scoped_call(d0, d1, a0) -> d0` — RAII scoped-temp wrapper: constructs a temp object on the stack, runs the real work through it, destructs it, returns the work's result.** Sets `a2 = sp+4` (`&temp`), calls ctor [`0x7e054741`](#) (stores vtable `0x7e3b3b0c` at `temp+0`, inits a sub-block at `temp+28` via the object-model op `0x7dfc070b`), then the worker [`0x7e0547fe`](#)`(d0, d1, a0=&temp, a1=orig a0)`, saves its `d0`, calls dtor [`0x7e05476c`](#) (releases the sub-block `0x7dfc0730`/frees via `0x7dfc68c1`/`0x7dfc698a`), returns the saved result. **Worker:** if `d0==0` returns early; else allocates (`0x7dfc68c1`), zeroes `temp+4`/`temp+8`, stores the int arg at `temp+24` and the ptr arg at `temp+12`, builds a structure (`0x7dfc4738`/`0x7dfc488a`/`0x7dfc076c`/`0x7dfc685d`/`0x7dfc698a`), then resolves `temp`'s own vtable method `*(*(temp)+4)` and `calls` it (dispatches the populated functor). A generic object-model runtime helper (the `0x7dfc`–`0x7e05` C++ class/container library, neighbor of [[sdk-api-console-7e02]]), not business logic. **42 callers all inside the object-model region: 18×`0x7e04`, 8×`0x7dff`, 8×`0x7dfe`, 5×`0x7e03`, 2×`0x7dfc`, 1×`0x7dfd`.** Dead end: the temp's class (vtable `0x7e3b3b0c`) and the dispatched method's purpose not resolved; stringless leaf. | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e1e85bd` | 42 | `evmgr_on_state` | **`evmgr_on_state(d0, a0=mgr, a1)` — Com_EvSenderManager "on state" entry handler: resolves the current state's handler object, traces it, and dispatches.** Reads the state count/index at `mgr+132`, gets the current index via `0x7dfc04b6`; if index beyond range returns. Otherwise builds a key on the stack and does a container lookup/insert through the object-model map ops `0x7e20765f` + `0x7e207732` (the `0x7e206`–`0x7e207` C++ container/allocator library), special-cases `mgr+120` (→ `0x7e1e87ac`), **traces `'*** on state %s ***'` (@`0x7e4264da`) via the trace sink [`0x7dfc01bf`](#)**, then releases the temp key (`0x7e2076c8`). Bank `0x7e1e` = **Com_EvSenderManager** ([[com-evsender-manager-7e1e]]); the `standbyState`/`shootModeState`/`functionModeState` mode machine runs through here. **42 callers across mode/event banks: 12×`0x7e1e`, 9×`0x7e0e`, 7×`0x7e0d`, 6×`0x7e1d`, 4×`0x7e1c`, 2×`0x7e1f`, +`0x7e18`/`0x7e0a`** (Wi-Fi remote/playback + event clients). Dead end: the specific state set keyed into the map and the `mgr+120`/`mgr+132`/`mgr+144` field roles not fully enumerated. | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e2bc8ea` | 42 | `guiapp_htbl_get43_d334` | **`guiapp_htbl_get43_d334(d0=idx, a0=obj) -> ptr` — another copy of the 43-slot handler-table accessor** (`idx < 43 ? *(obj+0x318+idx*4) : NULL`), byte-identical to [`guiapp_htbl_get43 0x7e2c21b3`](#) / [`guiapp_htbl_get43_da54 0x7e2c194b`](#) but in bank `0x7e2b` and serving yet another GUI App sub-state-machine: its paired broadcast iterator `0x7e2bc8ff` indexes the per-SM registry at base `0x6045d334`. Confirms the accessor+iterator family spans **both `0x7e2b` and `0x7e2c`** (the same bank as the 44-slot [`guiapp_sm_handler_get 0x7e2b8b9e`](#)). Iterator walks `obj+0x318` via `0x7e24ff2a`/`0x7e24ff41`, resolves vtable through `0x7e22be56`, `calls (vtable+4)` to broadcast. See [[guiapp-htbl-family-7e2c]]. **All 42 callers in-bank `0x7e2b`.** Dead end: this instance's state-machine identity (registry `0x6045d334`, stringless leaf). | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e2c194b` | 42 | `guiapp_htbl_get43_da54` | **`guiapp_htbl_get43_da54(d0=idx, a0=obj) -> ptr` — a second copy of the 43-slot handler-table accessor** (`idx < 43 ? *(obj+0x318+idx*4) : NULL`), byte-identical to [`guiapp_htbl_get43 0x7e2c21b3`](#) but serving a different GUI App sub-state-machine: its paired broadcast iterator `0x7e2c1960` indexes the per-SM registry at base `0x6045da54` (vs `0x6045db30` for the 0x7e2c21b3 instance). Bank `0x7e2c` holds **~18 such accessor+iterator pairs** (grep `add 792,a0`), one per GUI App controller; the iterator walks `obj+0x318` via `0x7e24ff2a`/`0x7e24ff41`, resolves each handler's vtable through `0x7e22be56`, and `calls (vtable+4)` to broadcast — the GUI App observer/notify dispatch. See [[guiapp-htbl-family-7e2c]]. **All 42 callers in-bank `0x7e2c`.** Dead end: this instance's state-machine identity (registry `0x6045da54` has no self-naming init string; stringless leaf). | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e2c21b3` | 42 | `guiapp_htbl_get43` | **`guiapp_htbl_get43(d0=idx, a0=obj) -> ptr` — bounds-checked accessor into the GUI App object's 43-slot handler/sub-object table at `obj+0x318`.** Returns `idx < 43 ? *(obj + 0x318 + idx*4) : NULL` (792 = `0x318`). Same shape as [`guiapp_sm_handler_get 0x7e2b8b9e`](#) (44-slot variant in bank `0x7e2b`); here in bank `0x7e2c` (GUI App Bluetooth-receive state machine, [[bluetooth-stack-7e6f]]), bound 43. The neighbor `0x7e2c21c8` iterates the same `obj+0x318` table (builds an iterator via `0x7e24ff2a`/`0x7e24ff41`, resolves the object vtable through `0x7e22be56`, then `calls` a method at `vtable+4`). **All 42 callers in-bank `0x7e2c`** (stringless leaf). Dead end: which 43 handlers/sub-objects populate the table (slot→role map) not enumerated. | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e2cd0a0` | 42 | `gui_set_state_pair` | **`gui_set_state_pair(d0, d1)` — stores two u16 words of GUI global state.** `*(0x6045e34c)=d0`, `*(0x6045e34e)=d1`. Paired getter `0x7e2cd08b(a1,a0)` writes the two words back through caller pointers. Callers pass small enum-like values (`(0,0)`, `(15,0)`), so `d0` reads as a mode/screen id and `d1` a sub-param — not coordinates. Sits in a cluster of GUI global-state accessors: getter/setter of `0x6045e35c` at `0x7e2cd0af`/`0x7e2cd0b8`, and a 31-entry command dispatcher `0x7e2cd0c1` (looks up `d2` in table @`0x7e593300`, stride 8, key `+0`/handler `+4`, `calls` handler, clears context `0x6045e344`/`0x6045e348`). **42 callers across GUI banks: 25×`0x7e2d`, 7×`0x7e2f`, 7×`0x7e2c`, 3×`0x7e2e`** (CCommon GUI/menu cluster, incl. GUI App [[com-evsender-manager-7e1e]] neighbors). Dead end: the exact meaning of the two state words undecoded (no self-naming string; small-int enum only). | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e6f3d66` | 42 | `obj_run_oneshot_cb8` | **`obj_run_oneshot_cb8(a0=obj)` — runs and clears a one-shot callback stored at `*(a0+8)`.** Loads `a2 = *(a0+8)`; if non-null, zeroes the slot (`*(a0+8)=0`) **then** indirect-calls it (`calls (a2)`) — fire-once finalizer/completion semantics (cleared before invoke so re-entry can't double-fire). Void. Lives among the olnet/BT object helpers: neighbor `0x7e6f3d77` pops/unlinks via `*(obj)=*(*(obj)+16)`, and `0x7e6f3d89`/`0x7e6f3d9f`/`0x7e6f3db5` are OlyQe event-flag signal wrappers over u16 ids @`0x608abb94..98` (`0x7d9a41fb`/`0x7d9a56b0`). **42 callers: 35×`0x7e6f`, 7×`0x7e6e`** — the olnet/Bluetooth networking object model ([[olnet-networking-7e6f]], [[bluetooth-stack-7e6f]]). Dead end: which role the `+8` callback plays (completion vs destructor) and the object layout not pinned — no self-naming string on this leaf. | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e6ffc64` | 42 | `olnet_trace` | **`olnet_trace(d0=level, a0=msg)` — conditional debug-trace emit for the olnet networking subsystem.** Reads the olnet trace-enable byte `*(0x60461f30)`; if nonzero and the level qualifies (`d0` defaulted to 1 when 0, then `btst 1,d0`), emits `msg` (a0) through the global debug sink [`0x7d996e85`](#). Enable byte set by `0x7e6ffb72`, read by `0x7e6ffb7b`. Callers pass `d0=-1` (force) + a message string: e.g. `'ip mode manual'`, `'already initialized'`, `'olnet_transport_stop'`; status dumps (`'dhcpd_state[%d]'`, `'--- I/F[%d] ---'`) are formatted via `0x7da82515` first. **42 callers: 38×`0x7e70`, 4×`0x7e6f`.** Banks `0x7e6f`–`0x7e70` = **olnet** = Olympus IP/TCP transport + DHCP server (Wi-Fi AP mode), the networking substrate under the Wi-Fi HTTP/CGI bank; distinct from the BT RFCOMM strings at 0x7e76 ([[bluetooth-stack-7e6f]]). See [[olnet-networking-7e6f]]. **Correction:** `0x7d996e85` (**3105 xrefs**) is the global debug/trace **output sink**, not a kernel critical-section as an earlier note implied. Dead end: exact sink ring-buffer mechanism and the `0xff/0xfe` sentinel handling in `0x7d996e85` not fully traced. | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7da93f99` | 43 | `mec_post_event_c3` | **`mec_post_event_c3(d0, d1, a0)` — packs a 12-byte MEC event message and posts it with command code 3.** memsets a 12-byte stack buffer, packs byte0 = `(d0 & 0x0f) | ((d1 & 0x0f) << 4)` (two 4-bit params), sets word `+4 = a0` (pointer arg), then calls the mec event-queue post `0x7da94157(d0=3, &buf)`. The poster `0x7da94157` takes OlyQe sem id 1 ([`0x7d9c580c`](#)), pulls a node from a pool (`0x7da94227`) and packs the 4-bit code into the top nibble (`asl 28`) of the node's first word before copying the payload. **All 43 callers in-bank `0x7da9`** = **`mec_ev.c`/`mec_timer.c`** MEC mechanical (shutter/mirror/motor/magnet) sequencer; sibling of [`mec_node_create 0x7da94275`](#). See [[mec-mechanical-7da9]]. Dead end: the code-3 command meaning and the two nibble params' roles undecoded (siblings bake other codes). | `symbol-map.md` |

List helpers: find-by-key `0x7da947b1` (`*(node)==key`), find/remove `0x7da947c9`. **All 43 callers in-bank `0x7da9`.** Bank `0x7da9` = **`mec_ev.c`/`mec_timer.c`** — the MEC mechanical (shutter/mirror/motor/magnet) sequencer with the `MS_*` state machine (`MS_MGWAIT`/`MS_BOUNDWAIT`/`MS_UPBREAK`/`MS_DOWNBREAK`/`MS_PIWAIT`/…) and `*_pre`/`*_post` phase events; below the capture stack ([[takepic-7db0]]→[[cam-driver-7db1]]→[[camsem-7db4]]). | `0x7dec6097` | 43 | `exp_halfev_to_lin16` | **`exp_halfev_to_lin16(d0=EV fixed-point) -> u16 linear scale` — exposure math: antilog of half the input, returning the middle 16 bits of the linear result.** Body: `x = sign16(d0); x = x / 2; lin = ev_antilog(x); return (lin >> 8) & 0xffff`. The core **`ev_antilog 0x7da8920b`** (wrapper) / **`0x7da89220`** converts a signed log-domain fixed-point value (units of 1/256 stop) to a `16.16`-style linear value: high byte → integer stops (applied as a right-shift in the wrapper), low byte → **256-entry 2^(frac) mantissa table at `0x7dbc0904`** (verified monotonic `0 → 65181 ≈ 65536`, i.e. `65536·(2^(f/256)−1)`), plus base `0x10000`; `x==0 → 0x10000` (=1.0). So the target produces a linear exposure/gain factor from a halved EV. Lives in the **exposure (EXP) calc bank `0x7dec`** (rodata `[exp]:calc_r/_s/_w …`, `calc_w ratio/ob/num/ch`, `resize`). **43 callers across the AE/metering cluster: 19×`0x7dd9`, 6×`0x7dec`, 6×`0x7dda`, 5×`0x7ddc`, 3×`0x7ddb`** (+ singletons `0x7e71`/`0x7e08`/`0x7dfc`/`0x7db6`) — banks `0x7dd9`–`0x7ddc` are the photometry/AE consumers of this EV↔linear conversion. Dead end: the exact photometric quantity (brightness Bv, gain, shutter factor?) the half-EV encodes, and why `/2`, not pinned; `ev_antilog`'s exponent-byte side output (`*(a0)`) unused here. | `symbol-map.md` |

First runs the 4-sided overlap test with sign-extended (`exth`) u16 compares: returns **0** (`clr d0`) if disjoint — `A.x1<B.x0` ∨ `A.x0>B.x1` ∨ `A.y1<B.y0` ∨ `A.y0>B.y1`. Otherwise writes the clipped intersection into the output rect — `out.x0=max(A.x0,B.x0)`, `out.y0=max(A.y0,B.y0)`, `out.x1=min(A.x1,B.x1)`, `out.y1=min(A.y1,B.y1)` (each via `cmp`+`ble`/`bgt` select-and-`movhu`) — and returns **1** (`mov 1,d0`). Leaf, `retf [d2,d3,a2,a3],32`. Immediately preceded (`0x7e23c83e`) by a tiny sibling that returns `A[6]==B[6]` (u16 eq at `+6`). Pure geometry helper in module `0x7e23` (CCommon utility layer) — no strings, no callees. **53 call sites: 23×`0x7e26` + 23×`0x7e23` + 3×`0x7e25` + 3×`0x7e22` + 1×`0x7e27`** (GUI/view layout + GSS clipping). | `0x7e6ff2a8` | 55 | `net_sock_op_checked` | **olnet socket-operation wrapper with errno check + diagnostic logging** (httpd/socket layer of the olnet TCP/IP stack). `net_sock_op_checked(d0, fd=d1, a0)`: `d2=fd`, `d3=0`, `a1=0`, calls the socket op `0x7e6ed7e2(d0,d1=fd,a0,a1=0)`; treats the result as **success when `ret == fd` or `ret ≥ 0`** → `return 0`; otherwise sets `d3=-1`, and reports the failure via the **errno→string logger `0x7e6ff4e5(errno)`** before `return -1`. Callee `0x7e6ed7e2` resolves `fd`→socket object through `0x7e6ede16` (null → **`-109` EBADF**), gates on object `+8` flag **bit16 (`0x10000`)**, and when set dispatches the recv-family op **`0x7e6ee2a2`** — which validates `a1≠0` (else **`-114` EFAULT**), `len≥1` (else **`-122` EINVAL**), connected flag `obj+8` **bit5 (`0x20`)** (else **`-157` ENOTCONN**), and shutdown bits **bit6/bit25** (else **`-158` ESHUTDOWN**). The logger `0x7e6ff4e5` maps negative socket errnos to the rodata tags **`EBADF_err`/`EFAULT_err`/`EINVAL_err`/`ENOTCONN_err`/`ESHUTDOWN_err`/`EAGAIN_err`/`EPIPE_err`/`ECONNRESET_err`/`EOPNOTSUPP_err`/`EIO_err`/`ENOPROTOOPT_err`** (`0x7e77c37c`+). **First symbol in the olnet httpd/socket module** (`0x7e6f`; rodata `httpd_send_err:I/O`, `send_error:header_send_err`/`body_send_err`, `<errorcode>%d</errorcode>` (`0x7e77c334`), `CGI No body root`, `parse_protocol_err`, `block_size_err`, `dhcpd_ini/stop/start fail`, `olnet tcpip dhcpd start …`) — the on-camera HTTP CGI server + socket API backing the Wi-Fi AP ([`wifi-api.md`](../usb-protocol/wifi-api.md)); sibling of the IP/WLAN interface layer ([`olnet_dbg_log_sd 0x7e6ffb84`](#)). | `0x7e6e2506` | 115 | `bt_lock_release` | **Bluetooth-stack recursive-mutex RELEASE** (unlock). Serializes the on-camera **Bluetooth subsystem** (module `0x7e6cxxxx–0x7e6exxxx`; identifying strings `"BTEVENT_CONFIRM_NUMERIC_REQ"`, `"BTEVENT_DISPLAY_NUMERIC_IND"`, `"SEC_SetPin err"`). Behavior: enter critical (`preempt_lock 0x7d9a16d7`), if `owner == self` then if `count==0` → clear owner, `resched_check 0x7d9a17c7`, **`OlySemaphoreSignal 0x7d9c16f9`(0x26)** (wake a waiter); else `count--`. RTOS service-call error tags **`OlyTaDD`** (`0x7E76E110`, enter) / **`OlyTaED`** (`0x7E76E118`, exit) via `rtos_svc_check 0x7d9c3e02`. **Acquire partner = `bt_lock_acquire 0x7e6e2478`** (row below; 101 xrefs). 115 callers across the whole BT stack (`0x7e6c/0x7e6d/0x7e6e`). **Note:** the E-M10 Mark I is Wi-Fi-only (no Bluetooth radio) — this is shared-codebase BT firmware for other OM-D bodies, present but not exercised on this body. First BT symbol mapped | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e6e2478` | 101 | `bt_lock_acquire` | **Bluetooth-stack recursive-mutex ACQUIRE** (lock) — exact mirror and acquire counterpart of `bt_lock_release 0x7e6e2506`. Same recursive state: **owner task id `*(0x7cc381a0)`**, **nest count `*(0x7cc381a4)`**, **OlySemaphore id `0x26`** (`*(0x7d9b03ca)`). Behavior: read self task id (`oly_cur_task 0x7d9a1c14` → `(4,sp)`), enter critical (`preempt_lock 0x7d9a16d7`); **if `owner == self`** → recursive re-entry: `*(0x7cc381a4)++` (bump nest count, no wait); **else** → `resched_check 0x7d9a17c7`, **`OlySemaphoreWait 0x7d9c1606`(id=0x26, timeout=-1 wait-forever)** to block until free, then `preempt_lock` again and set `owner = self` (`*(0x7cc381a0) = tid`). Always exits via `resched_check 0x7d9a17c7`. Same RTOS service-call error tags **`OlyTaDD`** (`0x7E76E110`) / **`OlyTaED`** (`0x7E76E118`) via `rtos_svc_check 0x7d9c3e02`. **Corrects the earlier "acquire sibling 0x7e6e2461" note on `bt_lock_release`:** `0x7e6e2461` (12 xrefs) is a *separate* BT lock on a different semaphore (`*(0x7d9b055a)`) + global (`*(0x60461ba8)`) via `0x7d9c106e`, not this one. 101 BT-stack callers; same Wi-Fi-only-body caveat as `bt_lock_release` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e6e12ea` | 71 | `bt_lock_enter` | **Bluetooth-stack critical-section ENTER wrapper** — the high-level bracket most of the BT stack uses instead of calling the raw lock directly: `bt_lock_acquire 0x7e6e2478()` (take the recursive mutex, owner `*(0x7cc381a0)` / nest `*(0x7cc381a4)` / OlySemaphore `0x26`), then increment the in-region counter `*(u32)0x7cc2cc44`. Returns holding the lock. **Close partner = `bt_lock_leave 0x7e6e12ff`** (83 xrefs): `*(0x7cc2cc44)--` then `bt_lock_release 0x7e6e2506()`. The counter `0x7cc2cc44` is touched **only** by this enter/leave pair (4 refs total, no reader in block-0) → a write-only nesting/outstanding-entry **diagnostic counter** (debugger/trace-inspected). 71 callers across the BT stack (25×`0x7e6e`, 18×`0x7e6c`, 15×`0x7e6a`, 13×`0x7e6d`). Same Wi-Fi-only-body caveat as `bt_lock_acquire`/`bt_lock_release` (shared-codebase BT firmware, no radio on this body). **Dead end:** semantic consumer of the `0x7cc2cc44` count — never read in block-0 | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e6d66db` | 56 | `bt_log_vformat` | **BT-stack formatted log/report builder into a fixed 10 KB scratch buffer, under lock.** Outer `bt_log_vformat(a1 = fmt from `(12,sp)`, varargs at `sp+16`)`: take BT lock [`bt_lock_acquire 0x7e6e2478`](#) (pairs with [`bt_lock_release 0x7e6e2506`](#) — both via task-id `0x7d9a1c14` + kernel mutex, checked by `rtos_svc_check 0x7d9c3e02`), run the formatter `0x7e6d66fc`, release lock, return. Formatter first **zeroes the whole 10240-byte (`0x2800`) buffer** at RAM `0x60862054`..`0x60864854`, then walks `fmt` writing into it: a `printf`-family parser — `%` (37) introduces a spec, handles conversions `s` (115, string), `l` (108, long prefix), width/flag digits `0`–`9` (48–57), and translates bare `LF` (10) → `CR` (13) on copy; `va_list` pointer at `(16,sp)` is fetched-then-advanced by 4 per argument. Emits a NUL at end of output. 56 callers all BT: **36×`0x7e6d`, 18×`0x7e6c`, 2×`0x7e6b`**. Same Wi-Fi-only-body caveat as `bt_lock_release`. **Dead end:** where the 10 KB buffer is flushed/consumed (log sink) and the full conversion-spec set not fully traced | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e6eaacb` | 57 | `bt_get_be16` | **BT-stack unaligned big-endian (network-order) u16 reader**: `return (u16)((u8)buf[0]<<8 \| (u8)buf[1])` (`a0`=byte ptr). Companion `0x7e6eaad9` = **get_be32** (`b0<<24\|b1<<16\|b2<<8\|b3`), paired with the LE readers `get_le16`/`get_le32 0x7e6eab06` and the put_* writers documented on the `bt_get_le16` row. Used to parse network-order protocol fields (vs BT's native little-endian). 57 callers all in-module: **26×`0x7e6e`, 23×`0x7e6c`, 5×`0x7e6d`, 3×`0x7e6a`**. Head of a **byte-stream serialization bank** used to parse/build BT (HCI/L2CAP/GAP) packet fields from unaligned buffers: siblings `0x7e6eab06` = **get_le32** (`b3<<24\|b2<<16\|b1<<8\|b0`), `0x7e6eab25` = **put_le16** (`buf[0]=v; buf[1]=v>>8`), `0x7e6eab31` = **put_le32**, `0x7e6eab4b` = **put_be16** (`buf[0]=v>>8; buf[1]=v`), `0x7e6eab57` = **put_be32**. 108 of its 108 callers are in the Bluetooth module (`0x7e6cxxxx` 16 / `0x7e6dxxxx` 83 / `0x7e6exxxx` 9), matching `bt_lock_release` above. BT is natively little-endian, so the LE accessors dominate; BE variants serialize network-order fields. Generic leaf (no strings); module identity from the exclusively-BT caller set. Same Wi-Fi-only-body caveat as `bt_lock_release` | `symbol-map.md` |

| `0x7e6eab71` | 107 | `bt_list_insert_after` | **BT-stack circular doubly-linked-list primitive bank** (head of the list library that sits right after the serialization bank above). Node layout: `+0` = prev, `+4` = next; the anchor/head is itself a node, so the ring is `anchor → first → … → anchor`. `0x7e6eab71` **insert_after**(`a0`=anchor node, `a1`=new node): `a1->prev=a0; n=a0->next; a1->next=n; n->prev=a1; a0->next=a1` (splice new after anchor). Siblings share the `retf [a2],4` tail: `0x7e6eab81` = **insert_before** (`a1->next=a0; p=a0->prev; a1->prev=p; p->next=a1; a0->prev=a1`, 28 xrefs), `0x7e6eab90` = **unlink_next**/detach (relink neighbours around the node, 46 xrefs), `0x7e6eab9d` = **pop/detach-and-clear** (unlink then zero the removed node's prev/next, 47 xrefs), `0x7e6eabaf` = **contains**(`a0`=anchor, `a1`=node) → walk `next` ring, `return 1` if `a1` found else `0` (63 xrefs). Bank ends before the object-init at `0x7e6eabc7`. All 107 callers in the Bluetooth module (`0x7e6cxxxx`–`0x7e6exxxx`; top prefixes `7e6dc`/`7e6db`/`7e6d0`/`7e6cb`), matching `bt_get_le16`/`bt_lock_release`. Generic intrusive-list leaf (no strings); module identity from the exclusively-BT caller set. Same Wi-Fi-only-body caveat — present in shared BT codebase, unused on this body. 3rd BT symbol family mapped | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e6eaca3` | 63 | `bt_status_byte_pack` | **BT-stack 2-bit status-byte packer** — head-visible entry of the **bit-accessor bank** that sits right after the list library (`bt_list_insert_after 0x7e6eab71`) in the same `0x7e6e` serialization region as `bt_get_le16 0x7e6eaaf8`. `bt_status_byte_pack(d0 = val, a0 = byteptr)`: clears `*(u8)a0`, **sets bit5 = 1** (always — a present/valid marker), then **sets bit4 = (val > 0 ? 1 : 0)**, via the bank's set-bit primitive. Bank primitives: **`set_bit 0x7e6eac8e`**`(a0=ptr, d0=pos, d1=val)` = `*(u8)a0 = (*(u8)a0 & ~(1<<pos)) | (val<<pos)` (read-modify-write one bit); **`test_bit 0x7e6eac7a`**`(d0=byte, d1=pos)` = `return ((byte>>pos)&1)` as 0/1; plus **2-D table-lookup siblings** `0x7e6eace2`/`0x7e6eacf8` (`idx d0*8 - 8 + d1*4` into byte tables `0x7e76eb10`/`0x7e76ec50` — small per-index curve/threshold blobs). Typical caller (`0x7e6eb4a7`) computes a derived bool `d0 = (d2 ^ 0x4445) & 1` and targets an object status field `obj+185`. All **63 callers in-bank (`0x7e6e`)**, matching the BT serialization cohort. Same **Wi-Fi-only-body caveat** as `bt_lock_release 0x7e6e2506` (shared-codebase Bluetooth firmware, no radio on the E-M10 Mark I). **Dead end:** semantic meaning of bit5/bit4 in the packed status byte (and the `0x7e76eb10`/`ec50` table contents) not pinned — stringless | `symbol-map.md` |

Flow: mark RTOS status via `0x7db931fe(code=0,d1=-1)` (same per-code status array `0x606fa5f4` as `req_dispatch_traced 0x7db930d6`); scan forward from `cursor+1` (wrap at 127) for a slot with `+36==0`; **if the scan wraps back to the cursor → pool full**: emit the error string via `0x7d996e85` and trap via `0x7d9c199e`. On success: set `slot+36=1`, build the slot handle through `0x7db92cff(idx)` (stored to the caller's out-ptr `a0`), advance cursor. Then tags the slot by the **request type `d3`** (orig `d0`): `d3==2` → `{slot+8=0x7d996fc6-ptr, slot+12=2048}`, `d3==1` → `{0x7d996f56-ptr, 128}`, else `{0,0}`. | `0x7db64630` | 124 | `errlatch_set_first` | **sticky first-error latch** of the `0x7db5xxxx–0x7db6xxxx` error-handling module: `u16 *p = (u16*)(ctx+92); if (*p == 0) *p = (u16)d0; retf`. Records error/status code `d0` into context `+92` only if the slot is still clear, so the **first** failure on a context wins and later ones don't clobber it. 124 call sites, each on a failure path, supply either a dynamic return code (`err=call …; if(err) errlatch_set_first(ctx,err)`, e.g. caller `0x7db60112`) or a fixed `0x3Cxx`-range error id (e.g. caller `0x7db5f5ba` latches `0x3C11`). Paired with the error-trace emitter `errtrace_emit 0x7db6480b`. `+92` is this module's context type (distinct from the many unrelated `+92` fields elsewhere) | `symbol-map.md` |

The loop consumes `nbits` across byte boundaries: computes `spill = bits_left_in_byte − nbits`; when `spill > 0` it packs the field inside the current byte with a built mask (`255 << k`, inverted, AND’d with existing byte) OR’d with `value << shift` and decrements the byte’s free-bit count; when `spill ≤ 0` it fills the current byte’s remaining bits, writes it, advances the byte pointer (`*(a0)++`), resets the free-bit count to 8, and continues with the leftover bits (`a2 = −spill`). Siblings: [`dosfat_tbl5_get 0x7de857db`](#), [`fs_log_record 0x7dea2b89`](#). **Dead end:** node struct semantics (`+4` type codes, `+6` index meaning, `+72`/`+76` flags) and the `0xFFF0`/type-2 case not fully pinned — stringless | `symbol-map.md` |

| `0x7db130d2` | 58 | `ret0_stub` | **shared `return 0` leaf stub** — 3 bytes, `clr d0; retf [],0` (returns `0`, takes no real args). Reused as a default/no-op method across the camera subsystems: all 58 call sites are direct calls (no vtable data ref), and every one loads an object into `a0` first (`mov a2,a0; call`) which the stub ignores. Serves as the "feature absent / nothing to do, report success (0)" handler, often selected after a mode/type check (callers `0x7daf8904`, `0x7dafa115` branch to it when a flag/`cmp` fails). Caller spread is cross-module: **26×`0x7db1`, 14×`0x7db0`, 5×`0x7daf`** (camera property/accessor layer) plus `0x7dec`/`0x7de3`/`0x7db4`/`0x7db7`/`0x7dd7`/`0x7deb`/`0x7dac`/`0x7e7c`. | `0x7d9a18fb` | 59 | `ectx_error_hook` | **empty E_CTX error-trap stub** — a single `retf [],0` (no args, no return value, does nothing). Every one of its 59 call sites (all in the RTOS kernel module `0x7d9a`) is the identical idiom: call inner service dispatcher `0x7d9a185b` → `cmp -25,d0` → on equal, `call ectx_error_hook` then `mov -25,d0` (propagate the error). So it is the **compiled-out debug trap/assert invoked when a µITRON kernel service returns `-25` = E_CTX** (service called from the wrong context — e.g. from an ISR / dispatch-disabled state). In a debug build this would panic or breakpoint; the release build leaves it an empty hook, so the wrapper just returns `-25` to the caller. Confirms the `-25 = E_CTX` decoding noted in [`krnl_svc_checked 0x7d9c1982`](#). **Dead end:** nothing to decode — it is intentionally empty; the semantics live entirely at the call sites | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d9c1982` | 254 | `krnl_svc_checked` | **checked RTOS kernel-service wrapper**. Calls worker `0x7d9a173b` then passes its return code to error-checker `0x7d9c3e10` (ctx ptr in `a0`=`0x7D9CDFC1`); if result `< 0` (`bge` fails) forces `d2=-1`; returns `0`/`-1`. Worker `0x7d9a173b`: reads **active IRQ-channel reg `(0x8050)`**, `asl2`-indexes per-channel control-block table base `0x8FF04F04`, dereferences block (fields +16/+36/+96), bumps a counter into `+36`, calls mask-ROM `0x47001b7f(-32768)`; returns **µITRON-style error codes `-25` (E_CTX, wrong context) / `-17` (E_PAR, bad param)** → camera RTOS is **µITRON-family**. Error-checker `0x7d9c3e10` (+sibling `0x7d9c3e02`): treat return `≥0` or `-50`/`-2` as OK, else report via `0x7d9c3e26`. 254 xrefs — pervasive kernel-call site | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7da89435` | 254 | `put_u16le` | **store u16 little-endian** into byte buffer: `(a0)=d0&0xff; (a0+1)=(d0>>8)&0xff`. Part of an 8-entry **endian (un)pack block** — all `(ptr=a0, val=d0)` byte-granular serializers (avoid unaligned word stores): `0x7da89428` `get_u16le` (`(a0) \| (a0+1)<<8`, u16); `0x7da89435` `put_u16le`; `0x7da89440` `get_u32le` (ret in d2); `0x7da8945d` `put_u32le`; `0x7da89474` `get_u16be` (`(a0)<<8 \| (a0+1)`); `0x7da89481` `put_u16be`; `0x7da8948c` `get_u32be`; `0x7da894a9` `put_u32be`. Used pervasively to pack/unpack protocol + file-format fields (PTP/USB, Wi-Fi, headers). 254 xrefs | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7db944b5` | 60 | `prop_write_grp8b54` | **property-group `0x8B54` WRITE accessor** — write sibling of the read accessor `0x7db9448c` documented in [`prop_op_dispatch 0x7db6bb60`](#). `prop_write_grp8b54(d0=id, d1=value)`: bounds-checks `0 ≤ (u16)id ≤ 810` (out-of-range → no-op `ret`), then calls [`reg_write_ctx 0x7d9d379b`](#) with registry handle `a0=*(0x606fa60c)`, key `d0=35668`(`0x8B54`), index `d1=id`, value `a1=d1` → stores `value` at element `id` of the `0x8B54` property group. | `0x7db6bb60` | 257 | `prop_op_dispatch` | **camera property/DB accessor dispatch** (3-arg form). Loads a function pointer from 2D fn-ptr table base `0x7DC01EE8` (stride **12 B/row = 3 methods per group**): row = group index `d0` (byte, `extbu`) ×12 via `udfu01 12,d0`, col = method index `d1` (byte) ×4 (`asl2`) → `table[row*12 + col*4]`, then `calls (a1)` passing handler arg = `a0` moved to `d2`, `exthu`→u16 in `d0`. Siblings: `0x7db6bb79` → table `0x7DC01F00` (stride 12, **2-arg**: extra `d1` from `a1`); `0x7db6bb95` → table `0x7DC01F18` (**1D**, row×4). Table targets = property accessors in `0x7db94xxx`/`0x7db6axxx` (first-row: `0x7DB9448C`, `0x7DB93FB5`, `0x7DB6A570`). Example handler `0x7DB9448C` bounds-checks id (`cmp 810,d1; bgt`→reject), maps to property key `0x8B54` (35668), uses registry RAM ptr `0x606fa60c`, calls accessor `0x7db94bd5`. Callers pass ids like 545/552/553 with col=2. Enclosing `0x7db6bba9` has a `d1` switch (1/2/3…). 257 xrefs — core property access path | `symbol-map.md` |

RAM globals note: cell `0x6044cf18` and the firmup scratch `0x6044cbc4` share the `0x6044xxxx` page →
that page is a **shared RAM globals/BSS region** inside the `0x60000000` RAM aperture (distinct from
the `0xa0000000` cached SDRAM data segment above).

## Heap allocator (core memory management)
The `0x7e206a0f`/`...a17`/`...a1f`/`...a27` thunk cluster (~6.6k combined xrefs) wraps the heap. Core:
| address | symbol | role | source |
|---|---|---|---|
| `0x7e208040` | `alloc` | tail-call → `heap_alloc` (size `d0` → ptr `a0`) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e20804a` | `free` | null-safe: if `a0!=0` → `heap_free`; manager at `0x60452220`, custom-free hook via vtbl `+16` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7db954ab` | `heap_alloc` | **size-class allocator**: buckets `≤124`→`0x7d996f56`, `≤2044`→`0x7d996fc6`, `≤16380`→`0x7d996f8e`, larger → further paths. Segregated-fit pools | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e206a1f` | `mem_free_thunk` | **1029 xrefs** — one-instruction tail-call to `free 0x7e20804a` (pointer in `a0`, null-safe). The standard free entry paired with the alloc thunks `0x7e206a0f`/`0x7e206a17`; `0x7e206a27` is an identical sibling. Dedicated row (was only noted inside the `0x7e206a0f` entry) |
| `0x7db954ed` | `heap_free` | matching free (pointer in `a0`): reads the **4-byte block header at `ptr−4`** holding the block's size, then dispatches by the same size-classes as `heap_alloc` (`≤124`→`0x7d996f64`, `≤2044`→`0x7d996fd4`, …) to return the block to its segregated-fit pool | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e206bfc` | 163 | `managed_obj_release` | **conditional managed-object release/teardown dispatcher** (heap-object lifecycle module, 163 xrefs). Marks the object in-teardown by `obj[+4] \|= 0x20000` (helper `0x7e207336`), then branches: if **pending-count `obj[+12] != 0`** (`0x7e20748c`) → invoke the object's own `vtable[+52]` (flush/defer), and when it reports more pending, call `vtable[+52]` again then `vtable[+4]` (deferred path — object not freed yet). Else (idle): if predicate `0x7e206f1d` (`obj[+52]!=0 \|\| global 0x604521f4!=0`) is false → run `vtable[+8](a1=obj+56)`; otherwise test `obj[+4] & 0x40000` (mask const `*0x7e42c570`) — if set and object non-null, run the virtual destructor `vtable[+0]` then a follow-up virtual call; if clear, **`operator delete 0x7e206a1f`(obj)**. Generic infrastructure (no class string anchors it); flag bits: `+4` bit17 (`0x20000`) = in-teardown, bit18 (`0x40000`) = has-custom-dtor-vs-plain-free; `+12` = outstanding-operation/refcount | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7db196d4` | 59 | `cond_node_signal` | **signal a bitmask into a keyed condition node and fire when complete** (cam condition-aggregator, facility **`0x0A093000`**). `cond_node_signal(a0=container, a1=mask, d1=id byte, d0)`: find the condition node by id via **`0x7db19a57`** (walks `container` list: head `*(container+8)`, node id byte `node[+0]`, next `node[+24]`); missing → [`cam_damage_record 0x7db19c09`](#)`(0x0A093000, line 891)`. On hit: **OR the caller mask into the node's accumulator `node[+12] \|= mask`**, then test completion `(node[+12] & node[+8]) == node[+8]` (all required bits now present, like the AND-mode of [`evflag_set 0x7db22d85`](#)); when satisfied, run post-hook `0x7db19a05(container, id)` and fire the report/callback through `0x7db4c246(container, id, node, handler 0x7db1977d)` (same logger/report helper `0x7db4c246` used by `evflag_set`'s sentinel path, global `0x606c9d20`). Returns `node[+12]`. 59 callers: 32×`0x7db4`, 27×`0x7db1` (in-module). A multi-source barrier: each source contributes bits for its id; the node fires once all `node+8` required bits arrive. **Dead end:** which sources/bits the ids name, and the post-hook `0x7db19a05` semantics, not pinned — stringless (only the `0x0A093000` facility tag) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7db22d85` | 241 | `evflag_set` | **set event-flag bits and release satisfied waiters** (RTOS event-flag-group `set`, 241 xrefs). Args `a0`=flag-group object, `d0`=bit pattern. Runs under IRQ lock `irq_save 0x7d9c580c`(IPL=2)…`irq_restore`. Requires valid byte `obj[+4]==1`. ORs the new bits into the group's flag word via helper `0x7db2314a` (`or_bits8`: `*(obj+8) \|= bits`). Then walks the waiter list head `*(obj+24)`, `next` at node`+12`, testing each with predicate `0x7db231ca` (`evflag_waiter_match`): mode byte node`+1` (`1`=AND-all → `(bits & mask)==mask`; else OR-any → `(bits & mask)!=0`), mask at node`+4`. On a match it decrements the waiter's pending-count byte node`+0` (sentinel `0xFF` = special/report node → `0x7db23238`, a trace/report wrapper via logger `*0x606ce3e8` / `0x7db4c246`) and, when the count hits 0, unlinks the waiter from the list (wake). Core scheduler primitive; pairs with the wait-side (not yet labeled) that builds these waiter nodes | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7db34313` | `smallmem_alloc` | **"Small Memory" fixed-block pool alloc** (263 xrefs; counterpart to `smallmem_free 0x7db344fd`). Under lock (`0x7d9c580c(1)`): pops the free-list head index from `0x606cf908` (u16); if `0xFFFF` the pool is exhausted → fatal `cam_damage_record 0x7db19c09` (code `0xA163000`, line 368). Else sets head = `entry[+2]` (next-link), marks `entry[+2]=1` (allocated) and `entry[+0]=size`, returns block address `*(0x606dbcac) + index*128`. Maintains used-count `0x606cf90a`++, high-water max-count `0x606cf90e`, max-size `0x606cf90c`. Table entry at `0x606CF924 + index*4` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7da9674c` | 155 | `pcnt_pool_free` | **fixed-block memory-pool block release** for the `[MEC_PCNT]` / `PCT_COM` pool (debug-dumpable "Small Memory / Large Memory" pool; sibling model to `lens_mem_free 0x7dac2a86`). Takes block ptr `a0`, classifies by address into one of two sub-arenas and frees under an **IPL=1 critical section** (`irq_save 0x7d9c580c(1)` … `irq_restore 0x7d9c5851`): **Small arena** base `*0x606ba6a4` (buffer `0x606b9084`), span `0x1200` (4608 B), **128-byte** blocks → 36 slots, status table `0x606b8fec`, free-list head `0x606b8fd8`, count `0x606b8fda`; **Large arena** base `*0x606ba6a8` (buffer `0x606ba694`), span `0x400` (1024 B), **512-byte** blocks → 8 slots, status table `0x606b907c`, head `0x606b8fe0`, count `0x606b8fe2`. Each status slot is a u16-pair `{link@+0, state@+2}`: free requires `state==1` (busy) else error (Small `0x1500b004`, Large `0x1500b005`); on success clears the slot, pushes the block index onto the arena FIFO (old head → slot link, index → head) and decrements count. Pointer in neither arena → error `0x1500b006`, handled by `0x7da91d8d`. Pool manager struct at `*0x606b8fcc` (field getters `0x7da96570`=+52, `0x7da9657e`=+56); init `0x7da965a7` seeds both bases + tables (Small 36, Large 8). Debug dump (`0x7da96836`, via `0x7da886fb`/`0x7da93a8a`) prints `=== Small Memory ===`/`=== Large Memory ===`, `Used Block/Size/Max`, `Area Size`, per-slot `USED/---- (%d) Size=%04d Adress=0x%08x`, tagged `PCT_COM`/`[MEC_PCNT] Client [0x%02x]`. **Dead end:** what this pool actually stores (picture-count / MEC component client data) not pinned statically | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7dac2a86` | 158 | `lens_mem_free` | **lens-memory block release / recycle** (158 xrefs, callers in the `0x7da9xxxx` lens cluster). Worker classifies the freed pointer `a0` by address into one of **two lens arenas** off base `*(0x606c4a84)`: `[base, base+0xF80]` (3968 B) = **`--lens_mem small--`** (descriptor `*0x606c4a88`), `[base+0x1000, base+0x7000]` (24576 B) = **`--lens_mem large--`** (descriptor `*0x606c4a94`); a pointer in neither range → error trace id `0x14009005` (`trace_log 0x7d997b14`). The arena descriptor is a **recycle FIFO** {read-idx `+0`, write-idx `+6`, buffer base `+8`}: `0x7dac2af4` tests full via `0x7dac2bdc` (`*(d+0)==*(d+6)` → trace `0x14009003` "queue full"), else pushes the block index via `0x7dac2bec` (`inc` write-idx `+6`, store). These arenas hold lens optical-correction data — neighbours in rodata: `depth`, `mtf_av/mtf_freq_num/mtf_freq_pos/mtf_capture/mtf_global` (lens MTF tables) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7dac29b7` | 146 | `lens_mem_alloc` | **lens-memory block acquire** — allocator counterpart of `lens_mem_free 0x7dac2a86`, same two-arena recycle FIFO. Worker selects the arena by requested size `d0` (u16): `0` → null; `≤128` → **small** arena (descriptor `*0x606c4a88`, pool `[*0x606c4a84, +0xF80]`); `≤8192` → **large** arena (descriptor `*0x606c4a94`, pool `[base+0x1000, +0x7000]`); `>8192` → further/error path `0x7dac2a44`. Pops a free block from the chosen arena's FIFO via `0x7dac2a5d` (empty test `0x7dac2bcd` → on empty, `trace_log 0x7d997b14` id **`0x14009002`** "queue empty" + null; else dequeue `0x7dac2c12`), then bounds-checks the popped pointer lies within that pool. Complements free's full-marker id `0x14009003`. 146 xrefs | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7dab6275` | 144 | `evtrace_record` | **binary event-ring recorder** — appends one fixed **20-byte** record to a **818-entry circular buffer**. Under `irq_save 0x7d9c580c(1)` … `irq_restore 0x7d9c5851`: entry ptr = `base + index*20` (index **`*0x606bb79c`**), fields `+0..3`=tag bytes (copied from `a0`), `+4`=**timestamp** (`0x7d996ba7` → `0x3FFFFFFF − SFR 0x98900074`, i.e. a free-running down-counter read as an up-counting tick), `+8`=`d0`, `+12`=`d1`, `+16`=`a1`; then `index++`, wrap to 0 at **818**. Init/reset `0x7dab6076` (clears enable, re-allocs the ~16360-byte pool). Callers (lens/command cluster `0x7da9xxxx`–`0x7daaxxxx`) wrap the call in a per-channel enable test `0x7daaf41f(ch)` and pass a 4-char event tag, so this is a lightweight lens/command **binary trace log** distinct from the text `trace_log 0x7d997b14`. **Dead end:** tag string vocabulary and which subsystem owns the 818-slot channel not fully enumerated (region is the lens-comm/command path; recorder itself is generic) | `symbol-map.md` |

On free: require status `==1` (else double-free), clear, push block index onto free-list head `0x606cf908` (entry`+2` = old head), decrement in-use count `0x606cf90a`. A second pool region is checked at `0x606dbcb0` (branch `0x7db34573`). Out-of-range or double-free → fatal **`cam_damage_record 0x7db19c09`** (code `0xA163000`, line 503/573). | `0x7d996fb8` | 143 | `scheap_large_free2` | **locked public free entry for the `scheap` large-block heap** — a thin per-worker thunk (`mov 0x7d9973a4,a1; call call_locked_arg 0x7d997031`) that runs the worker `0x7d9973a4` under the global heap critical section. Worker frees a pointer in the **large scheap region** (base ≈ `0xA0A53F40`, **27 blocks × 16448 B** stride `0x4040`): computes block index `(ptr − base)/0x4040`, range-checks `0..26` and exact alignment (nonzero remainder ⇒ invalid), validates the block is allocated via region table `0xA0A6A5A0` (`0x7d9975de`), then releases it (`0x7d9975f2`); on a bad/unallocated pointer it prints `"scheap_large_free2: bad pointer :"` / `"not allocated pointer :"` (`0x7d998b36`/`0x7d998b58`) plus the pointer via `dbg_print_hex 0x7d999a53`. ## Capability / flag tests
| address | xrefs | symbol | role | source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `0x7ddca1ce` | 1695 | `cap_test` | **`cap_test(mask=d0, group=d1)`** → `(*(0x60711d7c + group*4) & mask) != 0`. A 2-D capability matrix (group index × bit mask) in RAM table `0x60711d7c`. Sample sites: `(grp 4, mask 0x8000000)`, `(grp d2, mask 64)`. Candidate for the ROM capability family behind the ring-feature `descriptor_gate` | `symbol-map.md`, `community-dbfw-tool.md` |
| `0x7ddca28a` | — | `cap_group_of` | sibling that computes a capability `group` from an id (feeds `cap_test` as `d1`) | `symbol-map.md` |

RAM capability/flag table region: `0x60711xxx` (`0x60711d7c` matrix; neighbours `0x60711da8`,
`0x60711dac` read by the adjacent function) — inside the `0x60000000` RAM aperture.

## Object model — construction / dispatch / resolve
Corroborates the C++-style object model (vtable pointer at object `+0`; see `vcall_slot80`).
| address | xrefs | symbol | role | source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `0x7e2053c1` | 2182 | `OMReactive_ctor` | constructor of the **OMReactive** refcounted base class: writes vtable `0x7e42c10c` to obj `+0`, stores arg at `+4` (refcount), clears `+8` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e2053d2` | 2029 | `OMReactive_ctor_noarg` | same class, no-arg variant (installs vtable, clears `+8`, returns null `a0`) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e206dc9` | 950 | `OMReactive_ref_dec` | refcount decrement at obj `+4`; on underflow loads msg `"underflow in OMREactive less then 0"` (`0x7e42c580`) and calls assert stub | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e206de4` | — | `OMReactive_ref_inc` | refcount increment at obj `+4`; on overflow (`0xffff`) loads `"overflow in OMREactive too many active"` (`0x7e42c5b0`) and calls assert stub | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e2053aa` | — | `assert_stub` | **release no-op** (`retf` only) — the error-report target for OMReactive refcount asserts does nothing in production firmware | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7dff548d` | 2639 | `log_fmt_msg` | **CORRECTED** (was `obj_factory_512`): formatted-log builder — alloc 512 B scratch (`0x7e206a17`), `sprintf` with format string `0x7e385ebc` (`"%1s..%1s, %d..[HLS]: %1s"`), `console_out 0x7d996e85`, then free. The "class tag" was a **format string**, not a vtable | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7da80458` | 57 | `fp_pow` | **soft-float double `pow(x, y)`** in the C/soft-float runtime (module `0x7da8`, double-precision IEEE-754, NO hardware FPU). Args: base `x = {a0:a1}` (stored `(12,sp)/(16,sp)`), exponent `y = {d0:d1}` (stored `(20,sp)/(24,sp)`); builds constant **1.0** (`hi=0x3FF00000`) at `(4,sp)/(8,sp)`. Tests whether `y` is an integer via trunc helper `0x7da8075c` (extracts 11-bit biased exponent, bias 1023, zeroes result if `|y|<1`) + classifier `0x7da83ceb` (strip sign `&0x7FFFFFFF`, return signed code) — the integer-exponent / odd-even test needed for **negative base** handling (EDOM path sets errno). Core: `fabs`/sign-byte reader `0x7da801d1` (reads byte 7 of the double), `log` `0x7da801ef`, and double mul/decompose `0x7da83f80` compute `y·ln(x)`, then the result is range-checked against the two embedded `exp()` bounds — **`0x4086232BC07A...` = 709.782712893384 = ln(DBL_MAX)** (overflow) and **`0xC0862... = -708.3964185322641 = ln(DBL_MIN)`** (underflow). Overflow → `errno = 2` (ERANGE) at global **`0xa09f0984`** and returns +Inf (`hi=0x7FF00000 = 2146435071`); underflow → returns `0`. 57 callers, heaviest in **ADJ/factory-calibration** (`15×0x7e73`, `12×0x7e72` — gamma/power response curves), also `7×0x7d9e`, `6×0x7dab`, `4×0x7de0`, `3×0x7de3` (val-routing), `3×0x7dd5` (PLAY). Cousin of fast float convert [`float_to_i32 0x7da841d2`](#); shares classifier `0x7da83ceb` with soft-float `log`. **Dead end:** `0x7da801ef` (ln) and `0x7da83f80` (double mul) not separately labeled; exact EDOM/negative-base return path and the NaN-propagation constant (`0x7FEFFFFF`/`0x40E00000`=32768.0 seen mid-body) not fully traced | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7da841d2` | 58 | `float_to_i32` | **soft-float → signed-int32 convert (fast path)** in the C/soft-float runtime (module `0x7da8`, same block as [`sprintf 0x7da82515`](#)). Body is one op + return: `udf14 128,d0; retf [],0` — the vendor co-processor primitive `udf14 128` converts the IEEE-754 single in `d0` to a saturating int32 (returns `0x7FFFFFFF` on out-of-range), result straight out in `d0`. **No hardware FPU** on this core: nearby `0x7da84261` is a pure software float decomposer (`d0 & 0x7FFFFF` mantissa, `& 0x7F800000` exponent, `| 0x800000` implicit bit, `cmp 0x7F800000` Inf/NaN), and the checked sibling `0x7da841e1` does the same `udf14 128` then `cmp 0x7FFFFFFF,d0`→ falls back to the full path (`0x7da84261` + exponent math against `0x3F800000`=1.0f and `0x4F000000`=2³¹, NaN→0 via `d3` bit6, overflow→±). Variant `0x7da841d9` = same convert then `exthu` (→u16). 58 cross-module callers (`12×0x7ded`, `10×0x7e85`, `7×0x7ddc`, `6×0x7e87`/`0x7ddd` … — media/trace/metadata code doing float math), pattern `mov (12,sp),d0; call; mov d0,d2`. **Dead end:** exact `udf14`/`udf02`/`udf03` co-processor semantics not pinned (binutils decodes them as generic `udf*`); role inferred from the saturating-float-convert context | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7da82515` | 2099 | `sprintf` | **CORRECTED** (was `ctor_thunk`): variadic `sprintf(dst_desc=a0, fmt=arg, …)` → calls the format core `0x7da811b7`. (`0x7da82530` is a *different* thunk → `0x7da8254b`, not a format wrapper — see Open) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d9d36c4` | 2176 | `handle_to_obj` | resolve handle→object: if `a0==0` look up via `0x7d9d2bcc` (by id `d0`), else return `*(a0+4)` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d9d2bcc` | — | `handle_table_lookup` | **id → object-descriptor table lookup** (the registry behind `handle_to_obj 0x7d9d36c4`). Table base `0xA0A4354C`, **8-byte entries**, live-count halfword at `0xa0a43548`; `entry = base + id*8`, returns `entry+4` (object ptr) when non-null. Zero count or null slot falls through to a second-table path (`id*8` reindex at `0x7d9d2bef`) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d9dbe94` | — | `addr_region_classify` | **classify an address/value into a memory-region tag** by comparing against region bounds (`0x40000000`, `0x41000000`, `0x60000000`, `0x66000000`, …). Returns a small tag whose low 3 bits select access width in `reg_write_ctx` (tag `2` → 16-bit, `4` → 32-bit element). Used to pick the right store width for a handle's backing region (RAM vs SFR/shadow) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d9d379b` | 228 | `reg_write_ctx` | **context-aware indexed typed write through a handle** (228 xrefs). Resolves the target object (handle `a0`, else id → `handle_table_lookup 0x7d9d2bcc`, taking `obj+4`), classifies the backing region via `addr_region_classify 0x7d9dbe94` and masks the tag to 3 bits to choose element width, then stores the value at byte-index `d3` — region tag `2` does `movhu` to **two** destinations `(d3,a2)` & `(d3,a0)` (live + shadow copy), tag `4` scales the index to word stride. Picks an **IRQ-safe path** by the active-interrupt SFR `*(0x8050)`: in-IRQ → `0x7d9d3483` (guards with `0x47001b0a` in-IRQ check + `irq_save 0x7d9c580c`), task context → worker `0x7d9d37c5`. Sibling entry `0x7d9d380e` = same with the value/index args read from a different slot. A peripheral/SFR-shadow register-write layer keyed by logical handle | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d9d369a` | 126 | `handle_resolve_ctx` | **context-dispatched handle→object resolve** (READ-side sibling of `handle_to_obj 0x7d9d36c4`). `id = (u16)d0`; `obj = handle_table_lookup 0x7d9d2bcc(id)`; if null → return 0; else pass the descriptor's storage ptr `*(obj+4)` through the per-context dispatch core **`0x7d9d1cec`** and return its result (`a0`, used by callers as an object pointer, e.g. `0x7d9d21cf` then `cmp 0,a0`). `0x7d9d1cec` reads the active-context selector SFR `*(0x8050)` (the same IRQ/CPU selector `reg_write_ctx` keys on), indexes dispatch table `0x7d9df09c` by it, and `calls` that handler with the value — i.e. resolves/validates the object in the current (IRQ vs task) context. All `[CAPI]` log record fragments live at `0x7E78C2xx`–`0x7E78C3xx` (CSV `"%s","%s",%06u,%08u,%u,%s,"%s"`, `OpenSession:0x%X`, `File:%s`, `RecordWriteAdjNG`). 117 xrefs — the per-command adjustment-record writer. **Dead end:** opcode `0x902b` / type `0x2002` / key `0x2122` semantics not fully pinned (part of the CAPI adjustment command set) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e722d55` | 341 | `adj_dedup_record` | thin thunk (`clr d0; call 0x7e722bf2; extb d0; ret`) → worker **`0x7e722bf2`** on `adj_system` (`0x6091c53c`). Worker scans the dedup array at **+10920** (u32 × count) for the current value at **+8368**; on a hit it emits a record via `0x7e722a4d` (format str `0x7E78C2D0` `"%s","%s",%06u,%08u,%u,%s,"%s"` → CSV line), else appends the value and increments the **+12520** count (u16, capped at 400). `0x7e722a4d` = format-one-record: `0x7e758c20` (vsnprintf-like) into scratch **+9012**, then `strcat 0x7da836ab` onto accumulator **+8404**. Extends adj field map: **+10920 = dedup u32[≤400]**, **+12520 = dedup count**, **+9012 = format scratch**. 341 xrefs — inline "record current adj value" | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e7230f2` | 414 | `adj_block_ready` | `adj_system` (`0x6091c53c`) predicate: returns whether the **+9012 adjustment-data block** is applied/valid. Fast-path returns 1 if enable byte **+14**==0, or (+14 set AND state byte **+10042**!=0), or (+10043==0). Otherwise re-parses/validates the +9012 block (`0x7e758c20`, `strlen 0x7da8376d`, `0x7e7230a7`) and returns its result. Extends adj field map: +14 = block-enable, +10042/+10043 = parse-state bytes (cf. `adj_set_reg8368` +8368, +13 flag) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e72304c` | 94 | `adj_block_apply` | **`adj_system` (`0x6091c53c`) — start applying the +9012 adjustment-data block (async).** Active counterpart to the predicate `adj_block_ready 0x7e7230f2`. Steps: if enable byte **+14**==0 → return 1 (nothing to do); if parse-state **+10042**!=0 → first `adj_wait_pending 0x7e72314a` (drain the prior in-flight op); then reads **+10044 (u16 block id/length)** and posts the parse/apply request via the message-post primitive **`0x7e7c78d9`**(`obj`, id=+10044, mode `2`) — which allocs a msg object (`operator new 0x7d996f56`), stamps the id, and enqueues it to the adj worker (`0x7e7c7c68` scan). On post **fail** (`d0==0`): if **+10043**==0 set error state **+8 = 6** and return 0, else return 1; on **success** set **+10042 = 1** (mark pending/in-flight). Pairs with drain `adj_wait_pending 0x7e72314a` and predicate `adj_block_ready 0x7e7230f2` (which instead dry-run-validates via `0x7e7230a7`→`0x7e7c79ab`). 94 call sites across the `0x7e72xxxx`–`0x7e72bxxx` adj engine gate a step on re-applying the block. Extends adj field map: **+10044 = u16 block id/length** (handed to the parser). **Dead end:** exact contents of the posted message / what the worker parses from the +9012 block not pinned (no local rodata in this module) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e722d5f` | 94 | `adj_commit_record` | **`adj_system` (`0x6091c53c`) — commit the current adjustment command + record it.** Thin entry (`clr d1; call worker 0x7e722d69`) with OK-flag arg = 0; `(d0 = state code)`. Worker **`0x7e722d69`**: latches the current register value **`+4 = *(+8368)`**; if `d0!=0` sets **command-state `+8 = d0`**; reloads `+8` and runs the dedup-append worker **`0x7e722bf2`** (same as `adj_dedup_record 0x7e722d55` — scans +10920 dedup array, emits/append a CSV record); on a nonzero dedup result tail-calls **`0x7e722817`** which stores the flag arg into **result/OK byte `+0`** (`0x7e722817` returns 1 iff `+0==1`). So this path commits the command with **`+0` cleared (0 = not-OK / plain commit)**, distinct from the diagnostic OK-path writer `adj_record_write 0x7e722da4` (which runs when `+8==1`). The worker's other entry is inline in `adj_record_write` (`0x7e722e4f`). 94 call sites pass a state code and commit. Extends adj field map: **+0 = result/OK flag (1 = command OK)**, **+4 = latched reg value (snapshot of +8368)**, **+8 = command-state code**. Cluster: `adj_dedup_record 0x7e722d55`, `adj_record_write 0x7e722da4`, `adj_set_reg8368 0x7e7229ac` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e722839` | 219 | `adj_get_flag12` | leaf getter `return (s8)*(adj_system+12)` (`adj_system` = `*(0x6091c53c)`). The init/parse path `0x7e7227d0` seeds **+12 = bit0 of adjustment-data key `0x57A4`** (read via `0x7e723660`→`0x7e7235a8`→`get_adj_system 0x7e722617`), so +12 is a **0/1 enable flag**; every call site tests it as boolean (`extb d0; cmp 0,d0; beq`). **219 xrefs** — an adj-feature gate. Sits in the adj_system header-accessor block: sibling getters `0x7e722846` → `(s8)+13`, multi-out `0x7e722853`(out `a0`←+4 u32, `a1`←+8 u16, `a3`←+20 u16, `d1`-ptr←+36 u16); setter `0x7e722895`(+4=d0, +8=d1). Extends adj field map: **+4 = u32 header, +8/+20/+36 = u16 header fields, +12 = enable flag (key 0x57A4 bit0)** | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e7229ac` | 518 | `adj_set_reg8368` | `adj_system` register setter (singleton `0x6091c53c`): stores arg `d0` → field **+8368**, clears status bytes **+8372** and **+8404**, then re-inits via `0x7e759152` and flushes via `0x7e722a88`. The flush (`0x7e722a88`) is gated on byte **+13** and, when set, formats/copies the **+9012** adjustment-data block (`%f`/`%d`, via `0x7e758c20`/`0x7da836ab`). Sibling `0x7e7229f5`/`a02` = set/get paired reg **+8364** | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e724078` | 1490 | `ctx_f10916_dispatch` | gets `adj_system` via `get_adj_system 0x7e722617`, gates on byte field `+10916`, then calls `0x7e723f81` on sub-struct `+10148` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e7240a9` | 752 | `ctx_f10916_getval` | sibling of `ctx_f10916_dispatch`: same ctx (`0x7e722617`), gate byte `+10916` + readiness `0x7e723f81` on sub-struct `+10148`; if ready & `*(substruct)!=0` returns 64-bit pair (`d0:d1`) from `0x7da8484e`, else passes through caller's `d0:d1` default. Neighbor `0x7e7240e2` clears `+10916` (disable). So `+10916`=feature enable flag, `+10148`=feature state struct | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e205040` | 1255 | `base_reset_vtbl_7e42c02c` | lifecycle reset/dtor stage: installs base vtable `0x7e42c02c` at obj `+0`, then `0x7e2050d6` zeroes member at obj `+24` if non-null; returns null `a0` | `symbol-map.md` |

Known vtable constants: `0x7e42c10c` (**OMReactive** refcounted base class, built by `OMReactive_ctor`), `0x7e42c02c` (base vtable installed by `base_reset_vtbl_7e42c02c`), `0x7e42c09c`
(sibling vtable installed nearby at `0x7e2050e2`). The `0x7e42cxxx` block is a **vtable array** for one
class hierarchy.

| address | symbol | role | source |

| `0x7d9c132d` | `os_read` | generic blocking read/receive: `a0=handle obj (deref `(a0)`), d0=len/id, d1=timeout` (`-1`=block). **154 xrefs.** Dispatches to backend `0x7d9c4093` (timed) vs `0x7d9a7ba0` (blocking) after handle-validity check `0x7d9c3fa8`. Used by firmup (SD file read) + PC_PTP (queue drain) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d9c1429` | `os_chan_io_prep` | channel I/O prep (bidirectional): seek/prep before `os_read` in firmup; also the **send** path in `console_out` (buf, len, handle, timeout `-1`) — not read-only | `camera-fw-update.md` |
| `0x7d9b0858` | `console_out_chan` | console output channel handle used by `console_out 0x7d996e85` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d9c106e` | `OlyEventSet` | **OlyOS event-flag set** (187 xrefs): `(d0=event id, d1=bits)`. Picks ISR vs task kernel entry via context test `oly_in_isr 0x7d9c3fa8` → `0x7d9c410c` (ISR, label `"OlyEvStCpu"` `0x7D9EDD48`) or `0x7d9a682b` (task, `"OlyEvSt"` `0x7D9EDD53`); result via `oly_os_result_check 0x7d9c3e02`; error `"[OlEv]Err..EventSet ID :"` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d9c1138` | `OlyEventWait` | **OlyOS event-flag wait** (191 xrefs): `(d0=id, d1=wait bits/mode, a0,a1=out, +40=timeout)`; `timeout==-1` short-circuits. ISR vs task via `oly_in_isr` → `0x7d9c417a` (`"OlyEvWtCpu"` `0x7D9EDDA1`) / task path; error `"[OlEv]Err..EventWait ID :"` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d9c1606` | `OlySemaphoreWait` | **OlyOS semaphore acquire** (184 xrefs): `(d0=sem id, d1=timeout)`; `timeout==-1` short-circuits to the wait-forever path. ISR vs task context via `oly_in_isr 0x7d9c3fa8` → CPU-context entry `0x7d9c4275` (`"OlySmWtCpu"` `0x7D9EDEBC`) / task entry `0x7d9a8156` (`"OlySmWt"`), then the kernel wait primitive `0x7d9c3e10`. Return (in `a2`): **0 = acquired**, **-2 = timeout** (kernel `-50`), **-1 = error** → logs `"[OlSm]Err..SemaphoreWait ID :"` (`0x7D9EDECF`) with id + CPU ctx (SFR `0x8050`) via `0x7d9999f9`. Semaphore peer of `OlyEventWait` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d9c16f9` | 173 | `OlySemaphoreSignal` | **OlyOS semaphore signal/post** (173 xrefs) — release counterpart to `OlySemaphoreWait 0x7d9c1606`. `(d0=sem id)`. ISR vs task context via `oly_in_isr 0x7d9c3fa8` → CPU-context entry `0x7d9c4241` (`"OlySmSiCpu"` `0x7D9EDF05`) / task entry `0x7d9a6935` (`"OlySmSi"` `0x7D9EDF10`), then error-check `0x7d9c3e02`. On failure (`result < 0`) logs `"[OlSm]Err..SemaphoreSignal ID :"` (`0x7D9EDF18`) with id + CPU ctx (SFR `0x8050`) + current task id (`0x7d9a1c14`) via the OlyOS error formatter `0x7d9999f9` (also `"[OlSm]tID :"` `0x7D9EDEED` / `"[OlSm]CPU :"` `0x7D9EDEF9`). Returns result code in `d0` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d9a8454` | 71 | `wai_sem` | **µITRON `wai_sem` — NORTi-kernel semaphore acquire/wait** (the raw kernel service beneath the OlyOS `OlySemaphoreWait 0x7d9c1606` wrapper; called directly by NORTi-native code). `wai_sem(d0 = semid)`: resolves the current context TCB via the active-context SFR `*(0x8050)` indexed into the kernel context table `0x8FF00004`, **rejects calls from a dispatch-disabled / ISR context → `-25` (E_CTX)**; validates the id — group `(d0 & 0x7000)>>12` against the per-context max at `TCB+380` and number `(d0 & 0xFFF)` in `1..*(…+60)->[+6]`, out of range → `-18` (E_ID); resolves the semaphore OCB from the object table at `TCB+208` (`[number*4 - 4]`), null → `-42` (E_NOEXS). Then under dispatch-lock (`0x47001b7f` enter / `0x47001bfe` exit): **if sem count `OCB+24 >= 1` → decrement and return `0` (E_OK)**; **else block** — set TCB wait-factor `(2,tcb_wait)=4` (**TTW_SEM**), link the caller onto the semaphore's wait queue (`TCB+96`), and suspend. Error codes are standard µITRON (`E_CTX`/`E_ID`/`E_NOEXS`). Adjacent sibling service at `0x7d9a8540` (same prologue, TCB`+28`). 71 direct callers: 39×`0x7e6f`, 14×`0x7e6e` (Bluetooth stack), 11×`0x7e88`, 3×`0x7e87`, 2×`0x7e84` — NORTi-native subsystems. Part of the NORTi µITRON kernel wrapped by `ntos.cpp` (`BSE_*` semaphore service tags). **Dead end:** the id group/number encoding's exact object-domain layout not fully enumerated | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d9c3fa8` | `oly_in_isr` | OlyOS **execution-context test**: calls RAM kernel routine `0x47001b0a`; `==1` → in-ISR/non-task (logs, returns 0) so callers select the `*Cpu` kernel entry. Shared by `OlyEventSet/Wait`, `OlySemaphoreWait`, `os_read`, and peers | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d9c3e02` | `oly_os_result_check` | OlyOS call-result check: `d2<0` (error) → error-logger `0x7d9c3e26`; returns the code. Sibling `0x7d9c3e10` also whitelists `-50`/`-2` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7D9EDC80`–`0x7D9EDF00` | `olyos_label_pool` | OlyOS wrapper label/error-string pool: **`[OlEv]`** event (`OlyEvSt/Wt/Cl/Dl` + `*Cpu`/`i*` variants, `EventSet/Wait/Clear/Delete ID :`), **`[OlQe]`** queue (`OlyQeRd/Wr/Dl`, `QueueRead/Write/Delete`), **`[OlSm]`** semaphore (`OlySmWt/Dl`, `SemaphoreWait/Delete`). Confirms a full `OlyEv`/`OlyQe`/`OlySm` RTOS-abstraction API over the µITRON kernel | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7dfc0587` | `lock_get` | get lock object for id `d0`; lazy-inits the lock-manager singleton at `0x6044cf20` (alloc 44 B via `0x7e206a17` if null) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7dfc056a` | `mutex_lock` | virtual `lock()`: inner obj `*(a0+4)`, `vtbl=*(obj)`, `calls *(vtbl+12)` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7dfc057a` | `mutex_unlock` | virtual `unlock()`: inner obj `*(a0+4)`, `vtbl=*(obj)`, `calls *(vtbl+16)` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e02ec5a` | `get_gui_app` | **double-checked-locking** getter for the **GUI App settings** singleton `0x6044eb38` (1061 xrefs): if `*(0x6044eb38)` null, take lock id 2 (`lock_get`+`mutex_lock`), re-check, alloc 8 B + ctor `0x7e02ecb2`, store, release. Ctor `0x7e02ecb2`: obj+0 = **vtable `0x7E3A472C`** (code ptrs `0x7e02f…`; slot+0=`setSetting 0x7e02f92b`, slot+4=`getSetting 0x7e02f03c`), obj+4=0; then `0x7e05371c`→`0x7e02fd97` finish init. **CORRECTION:** earlier notes transposed the hex and called obj+0 a "static config record `0x7e473a2c` {15,15,…}" — that is a different, unrelated data address; obj+0 is the vtable `0x7E3A472C` | `symbol-map.md` |

## libc-style primitives
| address | symbol | role | source |
|---|---|---|---|
| `0x7da837b0` | `strncmp` | bounded compare: `a0=s1, a1=s2, d0=n`; returns 0 on equal-within-n or NUL, else signed byte diff. Used by `usr_checksum` id==2 path + region id-check | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7da837d4` | `strncpy` | bounded copy `a1→a0` up to `d0`, stops at NUL then pads (immediately follows `strncmp`) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7da83862` | `strstr` | **strstr** `(haystack=a0, needle=a1)` — returns pointer to first occurrence of `needle` in `haystack`, or `NULL` if absent; empty `needle` returns `haystack`, empty `haystack` with non-empty needle returns `NULL`. Outer loop finds a matching first char, inner `setlb`/`lra` HW-loop compares the rest; advances `a0` on mismatch. 103 xrefs. App-cluster `0x7da83xxx` libc (same bank as `strcmp 0x7da836f1`/`strlen 0x7da8376d`/`strncmp 0x7da837b0`); immediately precedes the `setjmp 0x7da8389e`/`longjmp 0x7da838b8` pair | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d997713` | `strcpy` | **strcpy** `(dst=a0, src=a1)` — byte-copy to NUL (`movbu`/`setlb`/`lne` loop), writes terminating NUL, returns the NUL ptr in `dst` (`a2`). 150 xrefs. Core `0x7d99xxxx` libc copy, sibling of `strcat 0x7d99772d`, `strncmp 0x7d997751`, `strcmp 0x7d997773`, `strlen 0x7d99779f`; distinct from app-cluster `strcpy 0x7da83710` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d99772d` | `strcat` | **strcat** `(dst=a0, src=a1)` — finds `strlen(dst)` via `0x7d99779f`, appends `src` bytes after it + NUL, returns `dst` (`a0`). Core `0x7d99xxxx` copy; cf. app-cluster `strcat 0x7da836ab` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d997751` | `strncmp` | **strncmp** `(a0, a1, n=d0)` — bounded byte compare, returns 0 when `n` bytes equal or both strings end, else signed `*a0−*a1` at first mismatch. Core `0x7d99xxxx` copy, sibling of `strcmp 0x7d997773` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d997773` | `strcmp` | unbounded byte compare `a0` vs `a1`; returns signed `*a0-*a1` at first mismatch, 0 if equal (574 xrefs). **Separate copy** in the core `0x7d99xxxx` region — distinct from the `0x7da83xxx` string cluster (two libc instances linked) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d99779f` | `strlen` | scan-to-NUL in core region (sibling of `strcmp 0x7d997773`); distinct from `strlen 0x7da8376d` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e7c926a` | 56 | `pool_free_banked` | **semaphore-guarded bucketed block-pool free/release by address.** `free(a0=ptr)`: null-ptr → return; bail if manager global `*(0x6046372c)==0` (pool uninitialized). Walks the **4-entry size-class band table** at RAM `0x6046377C` (stride 24, scanned high→low) to find the band whose base/bound bracket `ptr`, computing slot index `idx = (ptr − band.base) / band.step` via `divu`. Acquires pool lock **OlySemaphoreWait `0x7d9c1606`** (sem id `*(u16)0x7d9b0396`, timeout `-1` = forever), then searches the band's in-use slot array (`band+20`, indexed by `idx`) for the matching pointer, **clears that slot to 0** and bumps the band's free counter (`band+8`); releases via **OlySemaphoreSignal `0x7d9c16f9`** and returns. Band record fields seen: `+0` step/elem-size (divisor), `+8` free-count, `+16` region upper bound, `+20` slot-array base. 56 callers, `50×0x7e71` (the DPS/string-label module — [`strcpy_ret_len 0x7e7124f2`](#), [`dps_xml_emit_tag 0x7e719d3f`](#)) + `6×0x7e7c` (in-module). Pairs with an allocator sibling in the same `0x7e7c92xx` block (dup-path at `0x7e7c938c` re-acquires the same sem). **Dead end:** band table at `0x6046377C` is RAM, populated at runtime — exact size classes, the meaning of the fixed 4 bands, and whether entries are raw blocks vs handles not statically recoverable | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e7124f2` | 71 | `strcpy_ret_len` | **copy-if-nonempty string helper that returns source length.** `strcpy_ret_len(a0=dst, a1=src)`: `n = strlen(src)` (core `strlen 0x7d99779f`); **if `n != 0`** → `strcpy(dst, src)` (core `strcpy 0x7d997713`); returns `n` (`u16`, `exthu`). When `src` is empty (`n==0`) **dst is left untouched** — not even NUL-terminated — so it is not a drop-in `strcpy`; callers use the returned length to know whether anything was written. Thin wrapper over the two core `0x7d9977xx` libc primitives. 71 callers, concentrated in the `0x7e70`/`0x7e71` module (47×`0x7e70`, 24×`0x7e71`) — a string-table/label-building path that copies optional text fields and branches on the returned length | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e6e23f9` | `memcpy_thunk` | **memcpy thunk** (118 xrefs) — bare tail-call forwarding `(a0=dst, a1=src, d0=n)` to ROM **`memcpy 0x47001000`** (the mask-ROM libc at `0x47000000`). A per-module libc shim (one of a small thunk bank): neighbors **`0x7e6e241f` = memset thunk** (→ ROM `0x4700110c`), **`0x7e6e242a` = strlen** (inline `movbu`/`setlb`/`lne` scan, returns `a1−a0`; third copy alongside `strlen 0x7d99779f`/`0x7da8376d`). 118 call sites in this module reach memcpy through this local entry rather than the ROM address directly | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x47001000` | `memcpy` | mask-ROM `memcpy(dst=a0, src=a1, len=d0)` — 1394 xrefs | `memory-map.md` |
| `0x4700110c` | `memset` | mask-ROM `memset(dst=a0, val=d0, len=d1)` — 3612 xrefs (was mislabeled byte-copy) | `memory-map.md` |
